import math
from itertools import combinations

def get_cross_sections_at_z(spheres, z):
    """Get list of circles (center, radius) at height z"""
    circles = []
    for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
        # Distance from sphere center to plane
        dz = z - z_center
        # Radius of cross-section
        r_cross = math.sqrt(r * r - dz * dz)
        if r_cross >= 0:  # Valid cross-section
            circles.append(((x, y), r_cross))
    return circles

def get_connected_components(circles):
    """Get number of connected components of circles"""
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    n = len(circles)
    # Build adjacency list
    adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
    
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            center_i, r_i = circles[i]
            center_j, r_j = circles[j]
            
            # Distance between centers
            dist = math.sqrt((center_i[0] - center_j[0])**2 + (center_i[1] - center_j[1])**2)
            
            # Circles intersect if distance <= sum of radii
            if dist <= r_i + r_j:
                adj[i].append(j)
                adj[j].append(i)
    
    # Count connected components using DFS
    visited = [False] * n
    components = 0
    
    for i in range(n):
        if not visited[i]:
            components += 1
            stack = [i]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                if visited[node]:
                    continue
                visited[node] = True
                for neighbor in adj[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        stack.append(neighbor)
    
    return components

def find_critical_z(spheres):
    """Find all critical z-values where component count might change"""
    critical_z = set()
    
    # Add z values where sphere starts or ends (z = Z_i ± R_i)
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        start_z = z - r
        end_z = z + r
        if start_z > 0:
            critical_z.add(start_z)
        if end_z < 36000:
            critical_z.add(end_z)
    
    # Add z-values where spheres intersect (more precisely, where circles touch)
    # For each pair of spheres, find z where their cross-section circles touch
    for i in range(len(spheres)):
        for j in range(i + 1, len(spheres)):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # For circles to touch at height z, the distance between centers must equal sum of radii
            # Distance in xy-plane: d_xy = sqrt((x1-x2)^2 + (y1-y2)^2)
            d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2)**2 + (y1 - y2)**2)
            
            # Distance in z-direction: |z1 - z2|
            dz = abs(z1 - z2)
            
            # At height z, the radii of the circles are:
            # r1_z = sqrt(r1^2 - (z - z1)^2)
            # r2_z = sqrt(r2^2 - (z - z2)^2)
            
            # For circles to touch: d_xy^2 + (z - z1)^2 + (z - z2)^2 = (r1_z^2 + r2_z^2)
            # But r1_z^2 = r1^2 - (z - z1)^2, so:
            # d_xy^2 + (z - z1)^2 + (z - z2)^2 = r1^2 + r2^2 - (z - z1)^2 - (z - z2)^2
            # 2((z - z1)^2 + (z - z2)^2) = r1^2 + r2^2 - d_xy^2
            
            # Let A = r1^2 + r2^2 - d_xy^2
            A = r1*r1 + r2*r2 - d_xy*d_xy
            
            # If A <= 0, the spheres don't intersect at any z
            if A <= 0:
                continue
                
            # We need to solve: 2((z - z1)^2 + (z - z2)^2) = A
            # Expand: 2(z^2 - 2z(z1 + z2) + z1^2 + z2^2 + z^2 - 2z(z1 + z2) + z1^2 + z2^2) wait
            # Let me expand correctly:
            # (z - z1)^2 + (z - z2)^2 = z^2 - 2z*z1 + z1^2 + z^2 - 2z*z2 + z2^2
            # = 2z^2 - 2z(z1 + z2) + z1^2 + z2^2
            
            # So 4z^2 - 4z(z1 + z2) + 2(z1^2 + z2^2) = A
            # 4z^2 - 4(z1 + z2)z + (2*(z1^2 + z2^2) - A) = 0
            
            # Solve the quadratic equation
            a = 4
            b = -4 * (z1 + z2)
            c = 2 * (z1*z1 + z2*z2) - A
            
            discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
            if discriminant >= 0:
                sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                # Only valid if solutions are within [0, 36000]
                z1_sol = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                z2_sol = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                
                # Check if within valid range
                if z1_sol >= 0 and z1_sol <= 36000:
                    critical_z.add(z1_sol)
                if z2_sol >= 0 and z2_sol <= 36000:
                    critical_z.add(z2_sol)
    
    return sorted(critical_z)

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    # Find all critical z-values
    critical_z = find_critical_z(spheres)
    
    # Add boundary values
    if not critical_z:
        return 0, ""
    
    # Check if we need to add more critical points
    # The problem states that critical values differ by at least 0.01, so we have enough
    
    # Get component counts at each critical z-value
    component_counts = []
    
    for z_val in critical_z:
        circles = get_cross_sections_at_z(spheres, z_val)
        count = get_connected_components(circles)
        component_counts.append(count)
    
    # Determine transitions: 1 for increment, 0 for decrement
    transitions = []
    
    for i in range(1, len(component_counts)):
        prev_count = component_counts[i-1]
        curr_count = component_counts[i]
        
        if curr_count > prev_count:
            transitions.append('1')
        elif curr_count < prev_count:
            transitions.append('0')
        # If equal, no transition (we skip)
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)

    while True:
        line = input().strip()
        if not line:
            continue
        n = int(line)
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        spheres = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
            spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
        
        num_transitions, binary_transitions = solve_dataset(spheres)
        
        print(num_transitions)
        print(binary_transitions)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

In the year 2xxx, an expedition team landing on a planet found strange objects made by an ancient species living on that planet. They are transparent boxes containing opaque solid spheres (Figure 1). There are also many lithographs which seem to contain positions and radiuses of spheres.

<image>

Figure 1: A strange object

Initially their objective was unknown, but Professor Zambendorf found the cross section formed by a horizontal plane plays an important role. For example, the cross section of an object changes as in Figure 2 by sliding the plane from bottom to top.

<image>

Figure 2: Cross sections at different positions

He eventually found that some information is expressed by the transition of the number of connected figures in the cross section, where each connected figure is a union of discs intersecting or touching each other, and each disc is a cross section of the corresponding solid sphere. For instance, in Figure 2, whose geometry is described in the first sample dataset later, the number of connected figures changes as 0, 1, 2, 1, 2, 3, 2, 1, and 0, at z = 0.0000, 162.0000, 167.0000, 173.0004, 185.0000, 191.9996, 198.0000, 203.0000, and 205.0000, respectively. By assigning 1 for increment and 0 for decrement, the transitions of this sequence can be expressed by an 8-bit binary number 11011000.

For helping further analysis, write a program to determine the transitions when sliding the horizontal plane from bottom (z = 0) to top (z = 36000).



Input

The input consists of a series of datasets. Each dataset begins with a line containing a positive integer, which indicates the number of spheres N in the dataset. It is followed by N lines describing the centers and radiuses of the spheres. Each of the N lines has four positive integers Xi, Yi, Zi, and Ri (i = 1, . . . , N) describing the center and the radius of the i-th sphere, respectively.

You may assume 1 ≤ N ≤ 100, 1 ≤ Ri ≤ 2000, 0 < Xi - Ri < Xi + Ri < 4000, 0 < Yi - Ri < Yi + Ri < 16000, and 0 < Zi - Ri < Zi + Ri < 36000. Each solid sphere is defined as the set of all points (x, y, z) satisfying (x - Xi)2 + (y - Yi)2 + (z - Zi)2 ≤ Ri2.

A sphere may contain other spheres. No two spheres are mutually tangent. Every Zi ± Ri and minimum/maximum z coordinates of a circle formed by the intersection of any two spheres differ from each other by at least 0.01.

The end of the input is indicated by a line with one zero.

Output

For each dataset, your program should output two lines. The first line should contain an integer M indicating the number of transitions. The second line should contain an M-bit binary number that expresses the transitions of the number of connected figures as specified above.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have transparent boxes with opaque solid spheres inside
2. We're looking at cross-sections of these spheres at different z-heights
3. At each z-height, we get a collection of circles (cross-sections of spheres)
4. Circles that intersect or touch are considered connected
5. We need to find when the number of connected components changes (increment or decrement)
6. Each increment is marked with '1', each decrement with '0'
7. We go from z=0 to z=36000

## Key Insights

1. The number of connected components changes only at specific z-values where:
   - A sphere starts/exits the cross-section (when z = Zi ± Ri)
   - Two spheres' cross-sections touch or intersect (when the distance between centers equals sum of radii in the xy-plane)

2. For two spheres with centers (Xi, Yi, Zi) and (Xj, Yj, Zj) and radii Ri, Rj:
   - When the distance between their centers equals Ri + Rj, their cross-sections touch
   - The z-coordinate where this happens is: z = Zi + (Ri + Rj)² - (Xi - Xj)² - (Yi - Yj)²) / 2(Ri + Rj)

3. We need to find all critical z-values and sort them
4. For each critical point, check if the number of connected components increases or decreases

## Step-by-Step Solution

```python
import math
from collections import defaultdict
import sys

def distance_squared(x1, y1, x2, y2):
    return (x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2

def get_cross_section_z_values(spheres):
    """Get all critical z-values where connected components might change"""
    z_values = set()
    
    # Add z-values where spheres start/end
    for sphere in spheres:
        x, y, z, r = sphere
        z_values.add(z - r)  # sphere starts
        z_values.add(z + r)  # sphere ends
    
    # Add z-values where two spheres intersect
    n = len(spheres)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance between centers in xy-plane
            dxy_sq = distance_squared(x1, y1, x2, y2)
            
            # For intersection: distance between centers in 3D space - z difference
            # When the distance between centers in xy-plane plus z difference equals sum of radii
            # We need to solve: sqrt(dxy_sq + (z1-z2)^2) = r1 + r2
            # So: dxy_sq + (z1-z2)^2 = (r1+r2)^2
            # Therefore: (z1-z2)^2 = (r1+r2)^2 - dxy_sq
            
            if dxy_sq <= (r1 + r2) ** 2:
                # Spheres can intersect
                z_diff = z1 - z2
                rhs = (r1 + r2) ** 2 - dxy_sq
                
                if rhs >= 0:
                    # Two possible z-values: z1 - r1 + r2 and z1 + r1 - r2
                    # Actually, we need to solve for when the cross-sections touch
                    # The intersection occurs when the distance between centers equals r1 + r2
                    # So: sqrt(dxy_sq + (z - z2)^2) = r1 + r2
                    # (z - z2)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2 - dxy_sq
                    # z = z2 + sqrt((r1 + r2)^2 - dxy_sq)
                    # z = z2 - sqrt((r1 + r2)^2 - dxy_sq)
                    
                    sqrt_val = math.sqrt(rhs)
                    z_val1 = z1 + sqrt_val * (1 if (z1 - z2) > 0 else -1)
                    z_val2 = z1 - sqrt_val * (1 if (z1 - z2) > 0 else -1)
                    
                    # Actually let's be more precise
                    # We solve: sqrt(dxy_sq + (z - z1)^2) = r1 + r2
                    # Squaring: dxy_sq + (z - z1)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2
                    # (z - z1)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2 - dxy_sq
                    # z = z1 ± sqrt((r1 + r2)^2 - dxy_sq)
                    
                    sqrt_val = math.sqrt(rhs)
                    z_val1 = z1 + sqrt_val
                    z_val2 = z1 - sqrt_val
                    z_values.add(z_val1)
                    z_values.add(z_val2)
    
    return sorted(list(z_values))

def get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z):
    """Calculate number of connected components at given z"""
    # Get cross-sections at height z
    cross_sections = []
    for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
        if z >= z_center - r and z <= z_center + r:
            # The circle has radius sqrt(r^2 - (z - z_center)^2)
            d_z = abs(z - z_center)
            r_cross = math.sqrt(r * r - d_z * d_z)
            if r_cross > 0:
                cross_sections.append((x, y, r_cross))
    
    # Use union-find to find connected components
    if not cross_sections:
        return 0
    
    # For each circle, we need to check if they intersect
    # Two circles intersect if distance between centers <= sum of radii
    n = len(cross_sections)
    parents = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parents[x] != x:
            parents[x] = find(parents[x])
        return parents[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parents[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, r1 = cross_sections[i]
            x2, y2, r2 = cross_sections[j]
            
            # Distance between centers
            d = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)
            
            # Circles touch or intersect if d <= r1 + r2
            if d <= r1 + r2:
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

def solve():
    while True:
        n = int(input())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        spheres = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
            spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
        
        # Get all critical z-values
        critical_z = get_cross_section_z_values(spheres)
        
        # We need to check at critical points to determine transitions
        # Also need to check at boundaries
        z_values = critical_z[:]
        
        # Add boundaries
        z_values.append(0)
        z_values.append(36000)
        
        # Remove duplicates and sort
        z_values = sorted(list(set(z_values)))
        
        # We need to check the number of components at each critical point
        # and see if it increases or decreases between consecutive points
        transitions = []
        
        # Calculate components at each critical point
        components = []
        for z in z_values:
            comp = get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z)
            components.append(comp)
        
        # Check transitions between consecutive points
        for i in range(1, len(components)):
            if components[i] > components[i-1]:
                transitions.append('1')
            else:
                transitions.append('0')
        
        print(len(transitions))
        print(''.join(transitions))

solve()
```

```python
import math
from collections import defaultdict

def distance_squared(x1, y1, x2, y2):
    return (x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2

def get_cross_section_z_values(spheres):
    """Get all critical z-values where connected components might change"""
    z_values = set()
    
    # Add z-values where spheres start/end
    for sphere in spheres:
        x, y, z, r = sphere
        z_values.add(z - r)  # sphere starts
        z_values.add(z + r)  # sphere ends
    
    # Add z-values where two spheres intersect (their cross-sections touch)
    n = len(spheres)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance between centers in xy-plane
            dxy_sq = distance_squared(x1, y1, x2, y2)
            
            # For intersection: the distance between centers in 3D space equals r1 + r2
            # sqrt(dxy_sq + (z1-z2)^2) = r1 + r2
            # (z1-z2)^2 = (r1+r2)^2 - dxy_sq
            
            if dxy_sq <= (r1 + r2) ** 2:
                rhs = (r1 + r2) ** 2 - dxy_sq
                if rhs >= 0:
                    sqrt_val = math.sqrt(rhs)
                    # Two solutions: z1 + z2_diff and z1 - z2_diff
                    # Actually, we need to solve for z in the equation:
                    # sqrt(dxy_sq + (z - z1)^2) = r1 + r2
                    # (z - z1)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2 - dxy_sq
                    # z = z1 ± sqrt((r1 + r2)^2 - dxy_sq)
                    
                    z_val1 = z1 + sqrt_val
                    z_val2 = z1 - sqrt_val
                    z_values.add(z_val1)
                    z_values.add(z_val2)
    
    return sorted(list(z_values))

def get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z):
    """Calculate number of connected components at given z"""
    cross_sections = []
    for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
        if z >= z_center - r and z <= z_center + r:
            d_z = abs(z - z_center)
            r_cross = math.sqrt(r * r - d_z * d_z)
            if r_cross > 0:
                cross_sections.append((x, y, r_cross))
    
    if not cross_sections:
        return 0
    
    # Use union-find to find connected components
    n = len(cross_sections)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, r1 = cross_sections[i]
            x2, y2, r2 = cross_sections[j]
            
            # Distance between centers
            d = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)
            
            # If distance between centers <= sum of radii, they intersect/are tangent
            if d <= r1 + r2:
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count number of distinct components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

def main():
    while True:
        n = int(input())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        spheres = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
            spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
        
        # Get all critical z-values
        critical_z = get_cross_section_z_values(spheres)
        
        # Add endpoints
        critical_z.append(0)
        critical_z.append(36000)
        
        # Remove duplicates and sort
        critical_z = sorted(list(set(critical_z)))
        
        # Calculate number of connected components at each critical point
        components = []
        for z in critical_z:
            comp = get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z)
            components.append(comp)
        
        # Find transitions (1 for increment, 0 for decrement)
        transitions = []
        for i in range(1, len(components)):
            if components[i] > components[i-1]:
                transitions.append('1')
            else:
                transitions.append('0')
        
        print(len(transitions))
        print(''.join(transitions))

main()
```

import math
from collections import defaultdict

def distance_squared(x1, y1, x2, y2):
    return (x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2

def get_cross_section_z_values(spheres):
    """Get all critical z-values where connected components might change"""
    z_values = set()
    
    # Add z-values where spheres start/end
    for sphere in spheres:
        x, y, z, r = sphere
        z_values.add(z - r)  # sphere starts
        z_values.add(z + r)  # sphere ends
    
    # Add z-values where two spheres intersect (their cross-sections touch)
    n = len(spheres)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance between centers in xy-plane
            dxy_sq = distance_squared(x1, y1, x2, y2)
            
            # For intersection: the distance between centers in 3D space equals r1 + r2
            # sqrt(dxy_sq + (z1-z2)^2) = r1 + r2
            # (z1-z2)^2 = (r1+r2)^2 - dxy_sq
            
            if dxy_sq <= (r1 + r2) ** 2:
                rhs = (r1 + r2) ** 2 - dxy_sq
                if rhs >= 0:
                    sqrt_val = math.sqrt(rhs)
                    # Two solutions: z1 + z2_diff and z1 - z2_diff
                    # Actually, we need to solve for z in the equation:
                    # sqrt(dxy_sq + (z - z1)^2) = r1 + r2
                    # (z - z1)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2 - dxy_sq
                    # z = z1 ± sqrt((r1 + r2)^2 - dxy_sq)
                    
                    z_val1 = z1 + sqrt_val
                    z_val2 = z1 - sqrt_val
                    z_values.add(z_val1)
                    z_values.add(z_val2)
    
    return sorted(list(z_values))

def get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z):
    """Calculate number of connected components at given z"""
    cross_sections = []
    for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
        if z >= z_center - r and z <= z_center + r:
            d_z = abs(z - z_center)
            r_cross = math.sqrt(r * r - d_z * d_z)
            if r_cross > 0:
                cross_sections.append((x, y, r_cross))
    
    if not cross_sections:
        return 0
    
    # Use union-find to find connected components
    n = len(cross_sections)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, r1 = cross_sections[i]
            x2, y2, r2 = cross_sections[j]
            
            # Distance between centers
            d = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)
            
            # If distance between centers <= sum of radii, they intersect/are tangent
            if d <= r1 + r2:
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count number of distinct components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

def main():
    while True:
        n = int(input())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        spheres = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
            spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
        
        # Get all critical z-values
        critical_z = get_cross_section_z_values(spheres)
        
        # Add endpoints
        critical_z.append(0)
        critical_z.append(36000)
        
        # Remove duplicates and sort
        critical_z = sorted(list(set(critical_z)))
        
        # Calculate number of connected components at each critical point
        components = []
        for z in critical_z:
            comp = get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z)
            components.append(comp)
        
        # Find transitions (1 for increment, 0 for decrement)
        transitions = []
        for i in range(1, len(components)):
            if components[i] > components[i-1]:
                transitions.append('1')
            else:
                transitions.append('0')
        
        print(len(transitions))
        print(''.join(transitions))

main()
